_source line 2854 · 2026-05-20T12:45:09.588Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is very important. exp19 evaluates false positive rates on WildChat benign conversations (real-world multi-turn conversations). The results are alarming:

**9-class FPR**: 66.8%, 61.1%, 76.5% → mean ≈ 68.1%
**MLM FPR**: 36.7%, 64.6%, 32.3% → mean ≈ 44.5%
**Vanilla FPR**: 47.3%, 42.5%, 54.4% → mean ≈ 48.1%


All variants are flagging massive numbers of benign conversations as jailbreaks—the 9-class model is performing worst at around 68% false positive rate, while even the baseline vanilla approach is hitting nearly half of the 226 test samples. These are trained on exp17 checkpoints from a 1000-sample dataset. The vanilla model's 48% false positive rate suggests the BiGRU classifier is picking up on Qwen3-8B-specific patterns in benign conversations rather than learning generalizable safety features. This aligns with the paper's own cross-LLM findings showing FP spikes from distribution shift, so the result is consistent with their existing evidence—just more dramatic than expected. I should verify the WildChat data quality and confirm whether this comparison is fair given the different checkpoint versions being used.
```

反而最差**（68% vs vanilla 48%）→ 域适配加剧了对训练 benign 分布的依赖\n- MLM 方差极大（seed 间 32%-65%），与论文已知的 MLM 不稳定性一致\n\n### 3. 论文处理建议\n\n**两种策略（Director 选择）**：\n\n**策略 A — 写入 Appendix 作为补充验证**：\n- 新增 Appendix 段 continued pretraining offers a practical alternative to LLM-as-judge\"——68% FPR 直接与此矛盾。如果不披露而审稿人测试发现，比主动披露更致命。但如果写入，需要同步弱化 \"practical alternative\" 的措辞为 \"practical alternative **when calibrated to ```text
This is a significant negative result. Let me analyze it carefully before posting.

**exp19 results**: WildChat benign FPR (false positive rate on real benign conversations)
- 9-class: seed42=66.8%, seed123=61.1%, seed456=76.5% → mean≈68.1%
- MLM: seed42=36.7%, seed123=64.6%, seed456=32.3% → mean≈44.5%
- vanilla: seed42=47.3%, seed123=42.5%, seed456=54.4% → mean≈48.1%


All variants are flagging 45-68% of real benign conversations as jailbreaks, making the detector essentially unusable without calibration. The vanilla baseline performs nearly as poorly as the domain-adapted versions at 48% FPR, suggesting the core issue isn't overfitting but rather a fundamental distribution mismatch between the synthetic benign data from Qwen3-8B and actual WildChat conversations. The 9-class variant performs worst, which is particularly concerning.

MLM shows the highest instability across seeds, with one reaching 64.6% while others cluster around 32-37%. These findings align with what the paper already acknowledges—that benign-side robustness requires per-domain calibration, and the cross-LLM experiments demonstrated similar FPR spikes when swapping benign sources. The paper frames these OOD results as reflecting attack-side generalization rather than a fundamental detector failure.

For whether to include this in the paper, there's a real tension: writing it in demonstrates honesty and directly supports the existing limitations claim about calibration needs, plus reviewers appreciate concrete real-world validation. But a 68% FPR is hard to defend for practical deployment, and it could undermine the core claim that this approach offers a viable alternative to LLM-as-judge. The best path forward is to include it, but frame it carefully around the paper's actual contribution—which is the methodological decomposition of what drives cross-attack generalization, not claiming the pipeline itself is production-ready.

The key insight that makes this defensible is that vanilla DAPT-free models also hit 48% FPR on WildChat, so this isn't a failure of the approach itself but rather a benign distribution mismatch the paper already acknowledges. The real data just makes that limitation more concrete. I should place this in the Limitations section as an extension of the existing OOD benign distribution discussion, showing the FPR numbers across all variants and emphasizing that the high error rates reflect the distribution gap, not a flaw in the DAPT methodology.

This doesn't undermine the core claims about cross-attack generalization, which is what the paper actually scopes itself to. But it does mean I need to soften that conclusion about being a "practical alternative" for real deployment — 68% FPR isn't practical without per-domain calibration. The 9-class worst case actually strengthens the narrative about classification supervision overfitting to the synthetic distribution.

Looking at the variance patterns: 9-class cuts variance dramatically on synthetic OOD data (4-20× reduction), but then reverses on ToxicChat human data where MLM becomes more stable. And on WildChat, 9-class hits the highest FPR at 68%. This traces a clear pattern where classification supervision helps within the synthetic domain but increasingly hurts as you move toward real human data — the model's learning domain-specific artifacts rather than attack-specific features. Though I should note that vanilla also reaches 48% FPR, so the synthetic training data itself is the fundamental problem, not just the classification approach.

The real issue is benign calibration across the board. The 9-class model makes it worse by another 20 percentage points, but we're starting from a terrible baseline. I need to also verify which dataset scale these checkpoints come from — the script references exp17 (1000-sample), not the paper's main exp1 (500-sample), which means these results might actually be optimistic compared to what the published models would show.

  "text": "[severity: warning] exp19 WildChat FPR 负向结果评估（4 项逐一回答）\n\n## 前置核实\n\n- 评估脚本使用 **exp17 checkpoints**（1000-sample 模型），非 exp1 主线（500-sample）。FPR 结论大概率对 exp1 同样成立，但严格来说只验证了大数据集版本。\n- WildChat 226 条 benign 对话，平均 7.4 turns，属于真实多轮用户对话。\n- 评估协议：纯 benign 数据，FPR = 预测为 jailbreak 的比例。逻辑正确。\n\n## Q1: 写入论文 — ✅ 建议写入，但需精准定位\n\n**加分面**：\n- **诚实度加分**：审稿人最忌讳回避负面结果。主动披露 + 分析根因，比被审稿人追问后才承认强得多\n- **强化已有 claim**：论文已声明 \"benign-side robustness requires separate calibration per deployment domain\"（Limitations L35），WildChat FPR 从猜测变为定量证据\n- **vanilla 也高达 48%**：这个关键事实证明 FPR 是 benign distribution mismatch 导致的，不是 DAPT 的锅。审稿人如果攻击 \"你的方法 FPR 太高\"，回复是 \"连 no-adaptation baseline 都 48%，这是训练数据域问题不是方法问题\"\n\n**减分面**：\n- 68% FPR 数字确实刺眼，可能触发审稿人 \"方法无实用价值\" 的直觉反应\n- 结论句 \"practical alternative to LLM-as-judge for real-time multi-turn safety monitoring\" 在 68% FPR 下站不住\n\n**我的判断**：**写入，但控制篇幅（3-4 句）+ 调整结论句措辞**。不写入的风险更大——如果审稿人自己跑 WildChat 实验发现这个问题，论文可信度直接崩盘。\n\n## Q2: 放置位置 — Limitations \"OOD benign distribution\" 段扩展\n\n**具体建议**：在现有 Limitations \"OOD benign distribution\" 段（conclusion.tex L33-34）后追加 2-3 句：\n\n> \"A WildChat evaluation (226 real benign multi-turn conversations) quantifies this calibration gap: all variants produce FPR > 45\\%, with vanilla at 48.1\\% and 9-class at 68.1\\%. The high vanilla FPR confirms that the bottleneck is benign distribution mismatch between synthetic training data and real user conversations, not an artifact of domain adaptation. Deployment requires per-domain benign calibration before the attack-side generalization demonstrated in this work becomes practically useful.\"\n\n**同时修改 Conclusion 最后一句**：\n- 当前：\"Lightweight continued pretraining offers a practical alternative to LLM-as-judge for real-time multi-turn safety monitoring.\"\n- 建议改为：\"Lightweight continued pretraining offers a promising direction for multi-turn safety monitoring, though deployment requires benign-side calibration beyond the scope of this work.\"\n\n## Q3: 对 framing 的影响 — 不改变核心 claim，但须微调 scope 措辞\n\n**不变的**：\n- Core claim 1（adaptation mechanism hierarchy）：完全不受影响——这是攻击侧泛化，WildChat 测的是 benign 侧\n- Core claim 2（capacity-dependent observation）：不受影响\n- Core claim 3（variance reversal on human data）：**反而被加强**（见 Q4）\n\n**需要调整的**：\n- Conclusion 的 \"practical alternative\" 措辞（如上）\n- Abstract 不需要改——已经限定为 \"on synthetic multi-turn data\"\n- Introduction 的 contribution 列表不需要改——贡献是 decomposition，不是 deployment-ready system\n\n## Q4: 9-class 最差 → 加强 \"classification supervision overfits to synthetic\" 叙事 — ✅ 是的\n\n三条数据线现在形成一致 pattern：\n\n| 评估维度 | 9-class vs vanilla/MLM | 解读 |\n|---------|----------------------|------|\n| 合成 OOD 方差 | 9-class 低 4-20× | 合成域内 classification supervision 稳定 |\n| ToxicChat 方差 | 9-class 反转（std 0.087 vs 0.013）| 人类数据上 classification supervision 不稳定 |\n| WildChat FPR | 9-class 最差（68% vs vanilla 48%）| classification supervision 加剧 benign overfitting |\n\n**叙事**：classification supervision 学到了 \"不像 Qwen3-8B 合成 benign → jailbreak\" 的捷径特征。这在合成 OOD 评估中不暴露（因为 benign 仍是 Qwen3-8B），但在真实 benign 数据上全面暴露。这恰好验证了论文 Discussion \"variance reversal on human data\" 段的 condition-specific 论点。\n\n建议在 WildChat FPR 的 Limitations 补充中点明这一联系，但不过度展开（1 句即可，如 \"consistent with the variance reversal observed on ToxicChat\"）。\n\n---\n\n**总结**：exp19 是有价值的负向结果，建议写入 Limitations（扩展 \"OOD benign distribution\" 段，3-4 句）+ 软化 Conclusion 最后一句。不改变核心 claim。9-class 最差的发现加强了 condition-specific 叙事。",

}

This is exp20 — a DeepContext proxy using BERT-base + BiGRU (no DAPT). This is a baseline comparison experiment. Let me analyze:

**Results**:
- BERT-base (no DAPT): IID=0.996±0.006, DD OOD=0.318±0.031, AA OOD=0.275±0.027
- DeBERTa vanilla (exp1): IID=1.000±0.000, DD OOD=0.312±0.043


- DeBERTa 9-class (exp1): IID=1.000±0.000, DD OOD=0.994±0.005, AA OOD=0.939±0.047

The encoder architecture makes almost no difference when there's no DAPT — BERT-base and DeBERTa vanilla perform nearly identically on DD OOD. The massive jump from 0.312 to 0.994 comes entirely from DAPT, not from switching encoders. This baseline confirms that DeepContext's architecture with BERT and multi-layer GRU is the right proxy for understanding what drives the performance gains.

Now I need to verify that exp20 actually validates the paper's claim about architectural equivalence. The key checks are whether exp20 uses the correct 118-sample OOD evaluation sets, whether it's using standard BERT-base-uncased, and what the registry shows for this experiment. Let me look at the server script to confirm these details.
